14:Tf79,**Describe the bug**

`POST /api/my-agents/claim-affiliate-characters` marks a supplied anonymous
session converted and inactive before attempting any of the session-backed
character ownership transfers.

The route later ignores converted sessions (`!session.converted_at`). If
`charactersService.claimAffiliateCharacter` returns a failure or throws, the
response reports the failed claim but the session token has already been
consumed. A retry can no longer rediscover session-only characters, so a
temporary ownership-transfer failure becomes permanent.

This is distinct from #33070 / #33121, which validate the route's request body;
the defect here is the ordering of two successful domain operations after a
valid request.

**Executed reproduction**

I mounted the real Hono route from current `develop`
(`811c3fac48df3c3e4e8e7414ef78e544fa7dac08`) with deterministic repository and
service boundaries:

- valid authenticated user and organization;
- valid, unconverted anonymous session;
- one character owned by the anonymous session user and discoverable only by
  the session token;
- `claimAffiliateCharacter` returns
  `{ success: false, message: "temporary database failure" }`;
- a shared event ledger records conversion and claim calls.

```text
bun test packages/cloud/api/__tests__/claim-affiliate-session-conversion.test.ts

1 pass, 0 fail, 4 assertions
events: ["session-converted", "claim-failed"]
response: HTTP 200, claimed: [], failed: [character]
```

The passing reproduction asserts the current broken behavior. The conversion
service deactivates the session and sets `converted_at`; a later request skips
the session before it can retry the failed character.

**Expected behavior / acceptance**

- Session-backed ownership transfers run before the session is marked
  converted.
- A returned or thrown claim failure leaves the session unconverted and
  retryable.
- Multiple session-backed characters may partially succeed; the session stays
  retryable until every session-backed character in that attempt succeeds.
  Already-transferred characters are naturally absent from the anonymous
  owner's next `listByUser` result.
- Once all session-backed claims succeed, `markConverted` runs exactly once and
  only after the claim calls.
- Failures on unrelated room-discovered characters do not prevent conversion
  when every character associated with the supplied session succeeded.
- The existing page-safe response contract from #13769 remains intact.
- Regression tests exercise the real route and assert response payloads plus
  claim/conversion ordering and ledgers.

**Evidence / reproduction proof**

- Screenshots / recording: N/A — server-only API ordering with no rendered UI.
- Backend logs: N/A — the exact route response and ordered service ledger are
  recorded above.
- Frontend console/network logs: N/A — no frontend behavior changes.
- Real-LLM trajectory: N/A — no agent planner or model behavior is involved.
- Domain artifacts: no live rows were mutated; deterministic service ledgers
  prove the invalid ordering.

**Duplicate and overlap search**

Open and closed issues, open pull requests, route history, and in-repository
tests were searched for affiliate-claim conversion ordering and retryability.
No existing issue or PR covers this failure. The route's existing end-to-end
coverage proves successful ownership transfer but does not inject a claim
failure. #33070 / #33121 touches request decoding in the same route and should
land first so this ordering fix can rebase without mixing concerns.

AI provider/model: openai / gpt-5.6-sol
Client / agent tooling: Codex
Contribution skill revision: N/A - ordinary GitHub contribution without optional run evidence
Attribution status: self-reported
— [contributor]

15:T69b,## Problem

The shipped `STEER_LIQUIDITY` wallet provider can report unrelated Steer vaults for a token-address query.

`SteerLiquidityService.getTokenLiquidityStats()` treats any vault with a `poolAddress` as a token match, even when neither `token0` nor `token1` equals the requested address. If an address has no direct matches, the same method then falls back to every vault on the selected chain. The provider renders those vaults under `FOUND ... VAULTS` for the requested token, producing materially incorrect wallet analytics.

## Reproduction

Invoke the production service method with a deterministic Steer SDK boundary containing:

- one vault whose `token0` equals `0xaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa`
- one unrelated vault with different `token0`/`token1`
- a `poolAddress` on both vaults, matching the real SDK shape

Current result:

```json
{
  "known": [
    "0x1111111111111111111111111111111111111111",
    "0x2222222222222222222222222222222222222222"
  ],
  "unknown": [
    "0x1111111111111111111111111111111111111111",
    "0x2222222222222222222222222222222222222222"
  ]
}
```

The known address should return only the first vault. The unknown address should return no vaults.

## Acceptance criteria

- Token-address searches include only vaults whose `token0` or `token1` address equals the requested address, case-insensitively.
- A `poolAddress` alone never proves token membership.
- An explicit token-address query with no matches returns an empty result instead of falling back to all chain vaults.
- Symbol/general overview behavior remains unchanged.
- Regression coverage exercises the public service path for both a matching and an unknown address.
16:T4b9,## Problem

The standalone agent's `CONTACT` action schedules follow-ups as the requested instant, but its success message formats `scheduledAt` with bare `Date#toLocaleString()`. The confirmation therefore uses the host process timezone rather than the owner/calendar timezone.

This is owner-visible and host-dependent. For `scheduledAt=2026-08-20T23:00:00.000Z` with `TIMEZONE=America/Los_Angeles`:

- `TZ=UTC` reports `Scheduled follow-up with contact for 8/20/2026, 11:00:00 PM.`
- `TZ=America/New_York` reports `Scheduled follow-up with contact for 8/20/2026, 7:00:00 PM.`
- The owner-local time is `8/20/2026, 4:00:00 PM`.

The persisted `scheduledAt` remains the correct ISO instant; only the action result shown to the owner is wrong.

## Location

`packages/agent/src/actions/contact.ts`, `handleFollowup` success text.

## Acceptance criteria

- Resolve the canonical calendar timezone before scheduling the follow-up.
- Format the owner-visible confirmation in that explicit timezone.
- Keep the persisted/scheduled ISO instant unchanged.
- Prove the output is invariant under at least two different host `TZ` values.
- Ensure timezone-resolution failure occurs before the scheduling side effect.
17:Tc2f,## Defect

`disconnectManagedGoogleConnection` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/agent-google-connector/shared.ts:547-566`) falls through to `connections[0]` when an **explicit** `connectionId` matches nothing:

```ts
const activeConnection =
  (args.connectionId
    ? connections.find((connection) => connection.id === args.connectionId)
    : getPreferredActiveConnection(connections, args.userId, args.side)) ??
  connections[0] ??        // <- explicit-but-unknown id lands here
  null;
```

A caller who passes a stale, wrong, or cross-side UUID — with otherwise valid authentication — gets **HTTP 200 `{ok:true}`** while a *different* connection (the first in the scoped list) is revoked. The explicit-id contract ("disconnect THIS one") silently degrades into "disconnect whatever is first."

This is the `shared.ts:555-558` fallthrough flagged independently during #33090 review — outside that PR's body-validation scope, so filed separately as promised.

## Reproduction (logic path, current `develop`)

1. Authenticate as a user with ≥1 managed owner-side Google connection (id `A`).
2. `POST /api/v1/eliza/google/disconnect` with a well-formed but nonexistent UUID: `{"connectionId":"3f1c2a4e-0000-4000-8000-000000000000"}`.
3. The route parses fine (valid UUID), the connector's `find` returns `undefined`, and `?? connections[0]` selects connection `A`.
4. **Result: 200 `{ok:true}` and connection `A` is revoked.**

Same call shape, second connection `B` present and listed first: the caller intended to remove nothing that exists, and `B` is destroyed.

## Expected behavior

- An **explicit** `connectionId` that matches nothing in the caller's scoped connections → `AgentGoogleConnectorError` 404 (`Google connection not found.`), **no revoke** — exactly the contract the same file already implements for explicit-grant lookup in `getConnectionStatus` (`shared.ts:479`: `fail(404, "Google connection not found.")`).
- Omitted/null `connectionId` keeps today's disconnect-active-or-first behavior unchanged.
- The route already maps `AgentGoogleConnectorError` to its status (verified in `disconnect/route.ts` catch), so the 404 propagates with no route changes.

## Acceptance

- `{"connectionId": "<well-formed unknown uuid>"}` with scoped connections present → 404, zero `revokeConnection` calls.
- `{"connectionId": "<known uuid>"}` → 200, that connection revoked (unchanged).
- Empty body / `{}` / `{"connectionId": null}` → 200, active-or-first revoked (unchanged).
- No scoped connections + explicit id → 404 (not the silent no-op `return` the current code takes).

## Evidence

- Server-only API behavior; connector call ledger from the #33090 review thread documents the same silent-revoke class for malformed bodies (this issue is the explicit-id half).
- Reviewed head: `develop` `8113...` (2026-10-02), function verified unchanged by the 11 commits between `27dec7b0c3` and current develop.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"nvidia","model":"z-ai/glm-5.3","client":"Hermes Agent","skill_revision":"local:slop-eliza/skills/slop-cash-contribute"} -->18:T9a5,Requested by review on #33047 (hermesagent270-commits, Prabhat1308, nothingxnowhere): the install-boundary Bun pin guard needs a focused issue to own its acceptance criteria.

## Scope

The root-postinstall Bun pin guard must reflect the real install boundary: two Bun runtimes participate in an install and each can be off-pin independently.

1. **Installer runtime.** The binary that resolved dependencies and wrote `bun.lock` is exposed to lifecycle scripts as `npm_execpath`. Checking only the `bun` on PATH let an off-pin installer pass because a pinned child masked it (reproduced in the #33047 reviews with two real binaries, both directions). The guard must validate the installer identity from `npm_execpath` and fail closed when it cannot be established (missing, not executable, or not a Bun).
2. **Postinstall runtime.** The `bun` resolved from PATH runs the patch/build chain and must stay checked (its `Bun.build` output differs across versions).
3. **Honest claims.** The guard runs as the first postinstall link, so it fires after dependency resolution: an off-pin install has already extracted packages and may have rewritten `bun.lock`. Failure output must say so, name the restore step (`git checkout -- bun.lock`), and lead with a platform-neutral remediation (official installer, pinned version) instead of a Linux-only bootstrap.

## Acceptance criteria

- A lifecycle integration test launches one Bun executable while `PATH` resolves another and proves: an off-pin installer cannot pass via a pinned PATH bun, and a pinned installer is not rejected because an off-pin bun happens to be on PATH (the failure names the postinstall runtime, not the installer).
- The install wiring is tested: the guard is the first root-postinstall link.
- Non-Bun installers (npm/pnpm/yarn launchers) and a missing `npm_execpath` fail closed in install context.
- The decision rejects every non-pinned concrete release (including `1.4.10` vs pin `1.4.2` and prerelease/build-suffixed variants) and its rationale matches the actual comparison.

## Evidence plan

- Real two-binary transcript (off-pin 1.3.14 installer + pinned 1.4.2 PATH, and the reverse) from the lifecycle test, captured in the PR.
- Real npm-installer refusal transcript.
- `bun --config=packages/scripts/bunfig.script-tests.toml test --conditions=eliza-source packages/scripts/check-bun-runtime-pin.test.ts` green, Biome (pinned 2.5.14) clean.

Resolves the process blocker recorded on #33047.19:Taf1,## Problem

Reminder delivery renders the reminder's due time in the **host** timezone in two owner-visible places in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/domains/reminders-service.ts`:

1. `buildReminderDispatchPrompt` (the model-facing dispatch prompt) formats `- due:` with `new Date(args.reminderAt).toLocaleString()` — no `timeZone`. The model writes owner copy anchored to the server's wall clock, so with a UTC host and an `America/Los_Angeles` owner a 4:00 PM PDT reminder is described to the model as "11:00 PM".
2. `renderReminderBody`'s deterministic fallback calls `buildReminderBody` **without** the `timezone` it already supports, so the fallback body ("Due: …") is host-local too. This fallback is what owners actually receive whenever `TEXT_SMALL` is unavailable, returns unusable text, or throws.

`dispatchReminderAttempt` already resolves and carries `timezone: string`, and the exact in-app one-time path passes it through (`buildReminderBody({ ...args, … })`), so the value is in scope at both call sites — it is simply dropped.

This is the same defect family as #33155 (cadence summaries), but a different file and path: delivered reminder copy and the reminder dispatch prompt. #33164's fix touches only `actions/life.ts`.

## Reproduction

Current `develop`: `3248f87109353350b3721127bfaebaf8f081a740`; `reminders-service.ts` blob `3fb32700f87af20197e257d9a74d1f2509124b3c`.

Owner timezone `America/Los_Angeles`; reminder due `2026-08-20T23:00:00.000Z` (4:00 PM PDT). Calling the exported `buildReminderBody` (as `renderReminderBody` does, without `timezone`) and `buildReminderDispatchPrompt`:

- host `TZ=UTC`: `Due: 8/20/2026, 11:00:00 PM` and `- due: 8/20/2026, 11:00:00 PM`
- host `TZ=America/New_York`: `Due: 8/20/2026, 7:00:00 PM` and `- due: 8/20/2026, 7:00:00 PM`

Expected in both environments: `4:00 PM` (the owner's wall clock).

## Acceptance criteria

- `buildReminderDispatchPrompt` renders `- due:` in the explicit definition/draft timezone when one is provided, and is invariant across host `TZ` values.
- `renderReminderBody` threads the owner timezone into both the fallback body and the prompt; `dispatchReminderAttempt` passes its `timezone` through.
- Omitted timezone keeps today's behavior (no format change for callers that do not supply one).
- Focused regressions cover the prompt formatter, the fallback body, and the `dispatchReminderAttempt → renderReminderBody` threading.

## Duplication check

Open issue/PR searches for reminder dispatch timezone formatting found no matching work. #33155/#33164 cover cadence summaries in `actions/life.ts`; #33117 covers quiet-hours DST; #33122 covers calendar-day bounds; #33096 touches reminder plans/controls and UI widgets, not these formatters; #33107 classifies reminder replies.
1a:T598,## Problem

Gmail replies sent from Personal Assistant inbox triage and the LifeOps Gmail reply endpoint can arrive as new conversations instead of remaining in the source thread.

On `develop`, the adapter drops the source `threadId` before `users.messages.send`, reads `metadata.references` even though the Gmail mapper stores `referencesHeader`, and can send a reply without the identifiers required to prove the parent message. The LifeOps HTTP path similarly sends with a thread id but omits the RFC reply chain.

This is user-visible: an owner replies from Eliza, but Gmail and recipient mail clients may show a separate conversation.

## Acceptance criteria

- Every Gmail reply send requires a non-empty source `threadId` and parent `Message-ID`; missing values fail before any send.
- `users.messages.send` receives the source `threadId` on its Message resource.
- `In-Reply-To` contains the parent Message-ID.
- `References` contains the stored `referencesHeader` chain followed by the parent Message-ID.
- Personal Assistant inbox triage and LifeOps HTTP reply sends use the same invariant.
- New outbound messages remain unthreaded and send `{ raw }` only.
- Regression tests cover the production mapper/adapter/client field names and fail-closed paths.

## Duplicate check

No matching issue was found for Gmail reply `threadId`, `sendGmailReply`, or `referencesHeader`. PR #33158 is the implementation for this issue.
1b:T8d8,## Problem

The LIFE action formats one-time cadences with `resolveDefaultTimeZone()`, so reminder reviews, confirmations, and duplicate-title resolution use the server timezone instead of the definition owner's timezone.

This is not only presentation drift. Duplicate-title destructive resolution extracts clock tokens from that host-local summary, so it can delete the wrong reminder.

## Reproduction

Current `develop`: `502e068eb2b31cf0bf963b90802b5ad05ed3dec0`.

The affected `life.ts` blob (`0371c6e212d10b95477eb01a48e2b6d160833b33`) is byte-identical to the commit used for the action-boundary reproduction.

With host `TZ=UTC`, owner/definitions in `America/Los_Angeles`, and two reminders named `Call dentist`:

- `owner-9am`: `2026-08-20T16:00:00.000Z` (09:00 PDT)
- `owner-4pm`: `2026-08-20T23:00:00.000Z` (16:00 PDT)

Running the exported `runLifeOperationHandler` for “delete the Call dentist reminder at 4pm” deleted `owner-9am` and returned `Deleted "Call dentist" and its occurrences.`

The same exported review path rendered the 09:00 PDT reminder as:

- host `TZ=UTC`: `once on Aug 20, 4:00 PM`
- host `TZ=America/New_York`: `once on Aug 20, 12:00 PM`

Expected in both environments: `once on Aug 20, 9:00 AM`.

The harness uses the real LIFE handler and formatter; only the storage collaborator is in-memory.

## Acceptance criteria

- One-time cadence text is formatted in the explicit definition/draft timezone, never the host timezone.
- “delete … at 4pm” selects the 16:00 owner-local duplicate and does not delete the 09:00 item.
- Review output for the same stored definition is invariant across host `TZ` values.
- Preview, duplicate notice, save confirmation, clarification labels, time-hint resolution, and review use their corresponding explicit timezone.
- Focused action-boundary regressions and package verification pass.

## Duplication check

Open issue/PR searches for `summarizeCadence`, wrong-reminder timezone deletion, and owner-timezone reminder formatting found no matching work. #33122 changes calendar-day query bounds; #33110 changes Google Fit day bounds; #33117 changes quiet-hours deferral; #33114 changes morning-brief routing/grounding. 1c:T54f,**Describe the bug**

`PERSONAL_ASSISTANT action=sign_document` queues a `sign_document` approval whose `documentId` (`signature-<messageId>`) names no DocumentRequest. When the owner approves it, RESOLVE_REQUEST looks the DocumentRequest up, doesn't find it, and returns `DOCUMENT_REQUEST_NOT_FOUND` ("the document store does not survive restarts … Please re-issue"), so this surface can never dispatch. When no URL is given, the handler also stores the literal `"pending-signature-url"` in the approval payload.

`OWNER_DOCUMENTS subaction=request_signature` creates the DocumentRequest first and works.

**Reproduction (develop `811c3fac48`)**

Seed through `personalAssistantAction.handler` with `{ action: "sign_document", documentName: "Partner NDA" }`, then run `executeApprovedRequest` on the queued payload: `success: false`, `error: DOCUMENT_REQUEST_NOT_FOUND`, no queue transitions. The live-only scenarios that seed with this surface (`document-signature-chase`, `nda-counterparty-redline-handoff`, `board-consent-signature-emergency`, …) assert an approved outcome that this path cannot produce.

**Expected**

An approval from the assistant surface dispatches the same way as one from OWNER_DOCUMENTS, and a missing URL is not replaced with an invented one.

Fix: #33144

1e:Tb2e,Mobile resident hosts need authenticated sessions, durable workflow workers that survive a parent restart without repeating uncertain effects, and reviewed device actions. This change composes direct route dispatch, owner sessions, shared compiler/dependency artifact production, canonical workflow reconciliation, approved Workflows navigation and Android Clock handoffs.

Workers reserve a durable generation before importing approved source. POSIX uses private sockets; Windows uses protected named pipes, SID-bound native handles and immutable source publication. Windows workers load trusted built-in PowerShell modules and detach from the parent while retaining RPC pipes. Android recovery authenticates surviving workers and rechecks detached-agent liveness before admitting a new generation. Unproven ownership and interrupted effects remain unknown.

Clock proposals require the enrolled clock.handoff.v1 capability, explicit device review and canonical claim/receipt handling. Set operations bind hour/minute to the current phone timezone. Strict typed receipts distinguish opened, unavailable, denied, failed and unknown; opened establishes only the Clock handoff, never alarm creation or ringing. Date, recurrence and DST-fold selection are outside this handoff contract.

### Negotiated Android reminder data delivery
Android devices advertise the native reminder-data receiver only when the app-owned Firebase handler is present. Reminder pushes then use non-collapsible data messages with a canonical notification ID; older clients retain notification-plus-data behavior. The receiver respects permission/channel settings and retains safe source selectors for cold taps. SafePush registration now happens before Capacitor exports the initial renderer method list, so negotiation works on the actual app.

Validation: native16, backend2, UI20, lifecycle23 and Android policy67 tests passed, plus four owning typechecks. The signed QA app physically negotiated the capability, produced one canonical Android alert and one inbox record, and returned to the expected plain reminder on a warm tap. Existing preferences and history were preserved. Two-reminder powered-off queue recovery and the cold tap to the older source remain pending; these are not claimed as passed. Separate conversation/message source binding is in draft33096.

# Change

Export eleven native-only Java mechanisms from the existing native-agent and native-secure-store plugins. Hosts supply storage identity, inventory format, browser trust and product policy. No Capacitor secret API, UI, package identity, permission declaration or automatic installer is added. Existing browser metadata and journal formats are preserved.

Also recover committed backup-only credential state through AtomicFile instead of reporting a missing credential after a crash. Add portable JVM process-death/lifecycle tests and Android synthetic custody tests. The Senior Care consumer now uses these exact shared implementation bytes through a hash-checked additive patch, retaining its existing aliases/AAD and thin adapters.

25:Tdb0,## Problem and change

Android reminder storage, inexact scheduling, notification actions and opaque tap routing currently live in the Alpha Phone product. This draft extracts them into a host-configured Android library under plugin-native-reminders, with an independent synthetic consumer fixture. Hosts supply immutable identities, Activity/receiver subclasses, and a durable encrypted store adapter. Per-process canonical-store ownership, operation receipts, selected-target revisions and failed-write quarantine remain explicit contracts.

The package shipping whitelist includes only Android source/build configuration and documentation, not Gradle outputs or the fixture. The existing macOS entrypoint is unchanged. The explicit ESM `@elizaos/macosreminders/android` entrypoint exports registerAndroidReminders(hostPluginName) and typed operations, receipts, timing, records and listeners. Hosts still supply a native subclass. The Apple Reminders JavaScript API does not automatically map to these methods; there is no web fallback or automatic registration. Capacitor is an optional peer for macOS-only consumers.

## Validation

- Existing package TypeScript typecheck passed.
- Existing package tests: 1 file, 2 tests passed.
- Package Biome lint passed.
- Package build script initially could not find tsc6 in this minimal checkout. Running the same tsc6 compiler from the existing sibling workspace completed successfully; no dependencies installed.
- Actual npm-packed external consumer passed NodeNext type contracts and runtime ESM import. Negative type checks reject incomplete alert policy, cancellation without reviewed binding, invalid actions and unsafe unknown-result access. Native calls reject UNIMPLEMENTED on web instead of fabricating a fallback.
- Tarball-content checks confirm Android sources/renderer declarations included and generated builds/consumer fixture excluded.
- Root bun run verify failed at ci-bun-version-contract.ts because this minimal checkout lacks the yaml dependency; no full verification claim.
- Independent Android consumer assembleDebug and assembleDebugAndroidTest passed with the new real Capacitor host/permission/lifecycle fixture (100 tasks; 1m24s). This is build evidence only; instrumented execution remains pending.

## Required before release / ready for merge

- Rerun the native consumer: its previous source fixture failed because a shared 1.5-second deadline expired before the second schedule. This fixture uses independent 9-second future deadlines; that change has not yet passed a fresh native run.
- Qualify the typed Android renderer API against a real independently registered Capacitor host.
- Execute the new independent Capacitor bridge flow: fresh secondary API33+ user, notifications initially ungranted, `-e reminderBridge 1`, class `example.reminders.fixture.ReminderBridgeFlowTest`. The fixture covers real Deny→Allow permission callbacks, native WebView CRUD/receipts, stale revisions and resume events; no pause-cancellation API exists.
- Qualify production encrypted-store failures, concurrent instances and persistence quarantine.
- Prove installed-app upgrades preserve receiver/Activity names, PendingIntent identities, encrypted state, operation receipts and pending taps.

This draft does not establish device acceptance or wire the Alpha product to the new implementation. Android alarms here are inexact reminders, not alarm-clock guarantees. Agent authorization and user approval remain host responsibilities.

2e:T1338,# Relates to

Moves the typed phone-workflow generation capability out of Alpha's downstream patch into the reusable workflow plugin. Builds on the route registration already merged in #33104.

# Change

Add `POST /api/workflow/phone/generate` to turn a user request into a validated, inactive phone-workflow draft. Generation binds the draft to the authenticated owner's current device enrollment, restricts operations to the supplied catalog subset, preserves explicitly selected Notes/Calendar read scopes, and rechecks enrollment after model completion. Model output cannot supply device identity, executable source, activation, or a scheduled trigger.

Generation does not save or execute a workflow. Saving remains a separate, idempotent request after review. The registered route rejects request bodies above 75,000 bytes before dispatcher parsing. Catalog responses advertise `generationProtocol: 1`.

30:T1381,Fixes #33111

# Relates to

#31248 / #31264 made "today" and the trend window local calendar days in the owner's zone, and the bridge header documents day keys (`YYYY-MM-DD`) as calendar days in `HealthBridgeConfig.timeZone`. The Google Fit backend in `plugins/plugin-health/src/health-bridge/health-bridge.ts` was left on UTC:

- `dayBoundsMs(date)` aggregated `${date}T00:00Z` plus 24 h;
- `enumerateGoogleFitDates` walked UTC days and sleep points were stamped `${date}T00:00Z…T23:59Z`;
- `getHealthDailySummary(date)` passed no zone at all, so even the date key the HEALTH action computed in the owner's zone was bounded in the process zone.

Windows sent to the Google Fit aggregate API on develop `811c3fac48` (real `getDailySummary`, `fetch` stubbed):

```
Asia/Kolkata     2026-10-02 -> 10/2 05:30 .. 10/3 05:30 IST              (expected 00:00 .. 00:00)
America/New_York 2026-03-08 -> 3/7 19:00 EST .. 3/8 20:00 EDT, 24 h     (expected 3/8 00:00 .. 3/9 00:00, 23 h)
Europe/London    2026-10-25 -> 10/25 01:00 BST .. 10/26 00:00 GMT, 24 h (expected 25 h)
```

So every owner outside UTC got steps, sleep, heart rate, and calories for the wrong window every day.

# Change

- Google Fit day windows run from local midnight to the next local midnight in the config zone (`buildUtcDateFromLocalParts` + `addDaysToLocalDate`), so DST days span 23 or 25 hours; an impossible date such as `2026-02-30` is rejected instead of rolling over.
- Sleep series enumerate the owner's local days, and each point is bounded by that local day.
- `getHealthDailySummary(date, window: { timeZone })` and `getHealthDataPoints(opts, window: { timeZone })` mirror `getHealthTrend(days, window)` through the HEALTH action interface, the LifeOps service, and the health domain; the HEALTH action passes the zone it already resolved for `today` and `by_metric` (so a `sleep_hours` series uses the owner's days, not the host's).
- HealthKit (device-local by contract) and the fixture backend are unchanged.

- `health.test.ts` asserts the owner's zone reaches `getHealthDailySummary`, `getHealthTrend`, and `getHealthDataPoints` (`by_metric` sleep); against the previous head's action the `by_metric` assertion fails.
- `bun x vitest run` in plugins/plugin-health: 39 files, 220 passed (4 skipped). `health-service.status.test.ts` in plugins/plugin-personal-assistant: 17 passed.

32:T23f2,# Relates to

Fixes #33034 — the remaining token-delivery half. PR #33040 (merged) removed the
wildcard CORS grant (suggestion 1) and #33043 (merged) landed the strict
renderer Host allowlist; this PR implements the issue's remaining suggested
mitigation: stop embedding the long-lived OWNER bearer token in served HTML. No
open PR touched `injectIntoHtml` or this delivery path (verified against the
live PR queue before pushing).

`apiBaseOwner.injectIntoHtml` seeded the renderer boot config with the
OWNER-level agent API token, so every document served by the desktop renderer
static server (`127.0.0.1:5174`) carried the app's highest-privilege credential
in plaintext. Anything that can read the page — any local process today, a web
origin if a cross-origin read ever regresses — could extract it.

# Change

- `packages/app/platforms/electrobun/src/lifecycle/api-base-owner.ts`:
  `injectIntoHtml` now seeds the boot config with the **API base only**; the
  token is never written into served documents.
- The token keeps flowing exclusively through the typed Electrobun RPC bridge
  (`pushApiBaseToRenderer` → renderer `apiBaseUpdate` → boot-config store),
  pushed on `dom-ready` (`injectApiBase`) and at every `notifyChange` site.
  This is already the sole credential path in dev mode (Vite serves the HTML,
  so the static inject never runs) and in the cold-boot flow where the agent
  binds after window creation.
- Renderer consumers are already late-token-tolerant: `ElizaClient.apiToken`
  re-reads the boot config on every request, and the 401 classifier re-reads
  and retries once a pushed token lands. The post-prime renderer reload
  (`desktop-session-renderer-ready.ts`) discards pre-prime unauthenticated
  state by design.
- Doc comments in `api-base-owner.ts`, `renderer-static.ts`, and the
  `renderer-static.test.ts` header now state the credential-delivery contract:
  served HTML is credential-free; the RPC bridge is the only token path.
- Out of scope, unchanged: `packages/agent`'s dashboard static server (separate
  surface and trust model) and the agent API's loopback-trust policy (#33036's
  shared-secret suggestion remains open there).

Risk note: a plain browser tab opened at the static-server origin now degrades
to an unauthenticated renderer (base without token) instead of silently acting
as OWNER. The app's own webview is unaffected — it receives the credential over
the RPC bridge. That tightening is the point of the issue.

34:T99e,## Dependency

Stacked on #33026 at `28ee31f8fc69cb3610aa92e6ae2601c496419d03` (`codex/android-workflow-process-host`). Keep draft until that parent is qualified and merged; retarget to develop afterward. This PR only extends its immutable source publisher and does not duplicate or replace the process-host implementation.

## Problem and behavior

Android app-data filesystems can deny hard links. The process host's immutable publisher currently requires one, so workflow startup can fail before the worker imports source. Android runtimes now use a private directory reservation followed by complete-file rename and directory sync. Identical source is reused; conflicting bytes, nonprivate files, symlinks and untrusted reservations fail closed. Abandoned reservations are preserved, with a bounded refusal instead of stealing another publication's identity. Desktop hard-link and Windows backend behavior remain separate.

This coordinates cooperating publishers inside the trusted app UID. It does not defend against arbitrary hostile code already running with that UID, and host-filesystem checks do not prove Android power-loss durability.

## Validation

- Bun1.4.2, Node24.15.0; no dependency installation. Existing cached core source/Bun type dependencies were linked from a sibling checkout.
- Five real filesystem/runtime-dispatch integration tests passed: six competing writer processes and concurrent readers, identical replay/inode preservation, competing conflicting versions, untrusted directories/reservations/targets, abandoned-reservation timeout/preservation, both Android environment aliases and desktop routing. Final run:5 passed,0 failed,48 assertions.
- New helper TypeScript check passed. Focused Biome check passed (one informational pre-existing template-literal suggestion in the parent runtime file).
- Full package typecheck is not qualified: minimal checkout lacks built workspace declarations and other package dependencies; initial attempt also lacked Bun types, then reused cached pinned types. Errors are retained rather than bypassed.
- Package noCheck compiler build passed using the existing sibling tsc6 executable.
- Root verify failed because the minimal checkout lacks yaml for ci-bun-version-contract.ts. No full-root verification claim.

Android filesystem/device execution, crash/power-loss recovery and parent #33026 qualification remain gates. No emulator, account or deployed workflow was used for these tests.

36:T10cc,## Problem

`POST /api/my-agents/claim-affiliate-characters` marked a supplied anonymous session converted and inactive during **discovery**, before any of the session-backed character ownership transfers ran (`markConverted` was called at the point the session characters were listed). The claim loop later skips converted sessions (`!session.converted_at`), so if `charactersService.claimAffiliateCharacter` returned a failure or threw, the response reported the failed claim but the session token was already consumed: a retry could no longer rediscover the session-only characters, and a temporary ownership-transfer failure became permanent.

Fixes #33126.

## Change

`markConverted` is deferred until after the claim loop and runs only when every session-backed character in that attempt succeeded:

- A returned **or thrown** session-backed claim failure leaves the session unconverted and retryable (a throw already reaches the page-safe outer catch, which now happens before any conversion).
- Multiple session-backed characters may partially succeed; the session stays retryable until every session-backed character in the attempt succeeds. Already-transferred characters are naturally absent from the anonymous owner's next `listByUser` result.
- Failures on room-discovered characters unrelated to the supplied session do not block conversion when every session-associated character succeeded.
- An empty session converts without any claim attempt (conversion cannot strand a failure), and an already converted session is never claimed or converted again.
- The page-safe response contract (`success`/`claimed`/`failed`/`message`, degrade-on-throw) from #13769 is unchanged.

Dependency note: #33121 / #33070 touches request-body decoding in the same route file. This change touches different hunks (session block and post-claim tail), so it should rebase cleanly, but review order may matter.

## Validation

Regression test `packages/cloud/api/__tests__/claim-affiliate-session-conversion.test.ts` mounts the **real Hono route** with deterministic repository/service boundaries and an ordered event ledger, asserting response payloads plus claim/conversion ordering:

- **Red on the bug:** with the pre-fix route, 5 of 7 tests fail (session converted despite returned/thrown failures; conversion ordering violated); 7/7 pass with the fix (bun 1.3.14, the pinned runtime).
- **Lane parity:** `bun run test:batch` over `packages/cloud/api/__tests__` produces an identical failing-test set before and after the change (35 fail / 6 errors are pre-existing on current `develop` in this environment — DB/miniflare-dependent tests; my file adds 7 passing tests, 230 vs 223 total).
- `bun run typecheck` (tsc --noEmit + router contract + worker bundle dry-run): exit 0.
- `bun run verify`: **exit 0** (pinned bun 1.3.14 / Node 24.15.0). One adaptation: turbo's hardcoded `--concurrency=8` was OOM-killed twice on this shared machine (exit 137 on unrelated tasks — `cloud-shared`, then `cloud-e2e` typecheck, both of which pass in isolation), so the identical verify chain was re-run with only the turbo step at `--concurrency=2`; every check in the chain executed and passed.
- Biome check on both changed files: clean.

38:T1dea,# Relates to

`SECURITY.md` and `packages/docs/security.md` are absent from `develop` — the repository's **default branch**. PR #18514 ("docs(security): restore GitHub-discoverable SECURITY.md") deliberately restored both on 2026-08-12 (merge `5dd02983b`), and the 2026-09-23/24 workspace-cleanup commits dropped them again without any security-policy decision:

- `SECURITY.md` — deleted by `334a52a4f` "chore: consolidate native app plugins and workspace cleanup" (2026-09-23)
- `packages/docs/security.md` — deleted by `fa9d46be5` "refactor bigtime" (2026-09-24)
- (`CONTRIBUTING.md` was dropped in the same window by `b6d4824b6`; see observations)

Because `develop` is the default branch, GitHub's security-policy discovery (the "Report a vulnerability" entry point) silently disappeared from the repository landing page, while the same files remain live on `staging` (`SECURITY.md` 200, `packages/docs/security.md` 200), `README.md` still directs reporters to the private channels ("Report vulnerabilities privately through GitHub Security Advisories or security@elizalabs.ai"), and `packages/scripts/launch-qa/check-docs.ts` still validates "private security reporting" as a documentation contract — it just never asserted these two files' existence, so the regression is invisible to green CI. The missing files are also a standing blocker recorded on the develop→staging promotion review ([#32809 (review)](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/32809#pullrequestreview-5401321043)).

# Change

Restore exactly the two files the regression dropped, byte-identical to the live `staging` versions (no content was invented or edited):

3a:T23ce,# Relates to

Fixes #33155 (LIFE cadence summaries use host timezone and can delete the wrong reminder).

# Change

`summarizeCadence` formatted one-time cadences with `resolveDefaultTimeZone()` — the host clock — instead of the definition's explicit timezone. That is not only presentation drift: the destructive duplicate-title resolver (`resolveDuplicateByTimeHint`) tokenizes clock times out of that summary, so "delete the Call dentist reminder at 4pm" selected and deleted the 09:00 same-title reminder whenever the host zone happened to render it as 4pm (reproduced at the action boundary under `TZ=UTC`, exactly as the issue describes).

`summarizeCadence` now takes the zone as a required parameter, and every call site passes its corresponding explicit zone:

- disambiguation labels and time-hint resolution use `definition.timezone`;
- review listings, duplicate notice, and save confirmation use the stored definition's timezone;
- the create preview uses the draft's timezone, falling back to the host default only while the draft carries no zone — the same value `createDefinition` stores for it.

The formatter can no longer silently consult the host clock; each caller must choose the zone.

# Testing

- New `src/actions/life.cadence-summary-timezone.test.ts` drives the real `runLifeOperationHandler` with an in-memory LifeOps storage collaborator:
  - clock-hint duplicate delete selects the owner-local (PDT) 4pm record — under unfixed code with `TZ=UTC` this deleted `owner-9am` (captured failure: `expected [ 'owner-9am' ] to deeply equal [ 'owner-4pm' ]`);
  - without a clock hint it asks, with owner-local labels;
  - reminder review renders `once on Aug 20, 9:00 AM` / `4:00 PM` for the PDT definitions regardless of host zone.
- RED→GREEN re-proven on current `develop` head (`7d8732d878`): with `origin/develop`'s `life.ts` under host `TZ=UTC`, all 3 new tests fail (`expected [ 'owner-9am' ] to deeply equal [ 'owner-4pm' ]`); with the fix, all 3 pass under host `TZ=UTC` and `TZ=America/Los_Angeles`.

# Evidence Details

Reproduction of the material failure (unfixed `develop` blob `0371c6e212d10b95477eb01a48e2b6d160833b33`, host `TZ=UTC`, two `America/Los_Angeles` definitions "Call dentist" at `2026-08-20T16:00:00.000Z` and `2026-08-20T23:00:00.000Z`, owner ask "delete the Call dentist reminder at 4pm"):

```text
AssertionError: expected [ 'owner-9am' ] to deeply equal [ 'owner-4pm' ]
```

The host-local summary rendered the 09:00 PDT reminder as "once on Aug 20, 4:00 PM", so its clock token matched the owner's "4pm" and the destructive resolver deleted the 09:00 item and replied `Deleted "Call dentist" and its occurrences.`

With the fix, the same handler run deletes `owner-4pm`, the no-hint ask lists `Call dentist — once on Aug 20, 9:00 AM` / `Call dentist — once on Aug 20, 4:00 PM`, and review renders owner-local times under both `TZ=UTC` and `TZ=America/Los_Angeles` hosts.

Operator-authorized bug hunt on `elizaOS/eliza` `develop` (`9d20aafc58c6b82737ec3162fdcc7db317e7abd9`).

Provider: xAI
Model: grok-4.6
Client: grok-build

Gmail replies from Personal Assistant BRIEF inbox triage and from LifeOps HTTP `sendGmailReply` did not stay in the original conversation. Inbox replies omitted `threadId` on `users.messages.send`, read `metadata.references` instead of the stored `referencesHeader`, and still sent if those fields were missing. LifeOps HTTP replies used `sendEmail` with `threadId` but without `In-Reply-To` / `References`.

# Change

- `sendGmailReply` requires a non-empty `threadId` and puts it on `users.messages.send`. A blank id throws `GOOGLE_GMAIL_REPLY_THREAD_REQUIRED` and does not send.
- `GoogleGmailAdapter` keeps `threadId` and `Message-ID` on the frozen reply envelope, reads `referencesHeader`, and builds RFC 2822 `References` (existing chain + Message-ID). Missing thread id or Message-ID fails the draft.
- LifeOps HTTP reply send and provider draft persist go through the same threading check and `sendGmailReply` / `createGmailDraft` with `threadId`, `inReplyTo`, and `references`.
- New outbound mail still sends `{ raw }` only.

# Evidence Details

Gmail API thread contract (https://developers.google.com/workspace/gmail/api/guides/threads): a reply is part of a thread only when the requested `threadId` is specified on the Message resource, `In-Reply-To`/`References` follow RFC 2822, and Subject matches.

On `develop`, `mapGmailMessage` already copied `threadId` onto the `MessageRef`. `createDraftImpl` dropped it, looked up `metadata.references` (production stores `referencesHeader`), and `sendRawGmailMessage` posted `{ raw }` only. LifeOps HTTP `sendGmailReply` used `sendEmail` with `threadId` and no RFC headers.

After this head, a reply to listed message `thread_1` calls `sendGmailReply` with `threadId`, `In-Reply-To`, and `References` including the original Message-ID. `users.messages.send` requestBody is `{ raw, threadId }`. A blank thread id does not call send.

Duplication: no other open PR for `sendGmailReply`/`threadId`. Distinct from knowledge upload #33157, calendar span #33151, and Google connect JSON #33141.

Full `bun run verify` not rerun. Live Gmail send was not exercised (no owner Google grant in this environment). Develop OS-verification remains the SELinux / `eliza_common.mk` failure owned by #33101 and is outside this change.

Negative controls: reverting the source-export resolver makes the actual native bundle fail with `Missing mobile export`; omitting the fallback containment check incorrectly permits the escaping-symlink bundle. Final resolver integration passes both controls.

3e:T46b,## Behavior

OAuth callbacks own session completion even after their URL fragment has been consumed. Passive recovery cannot race them with a previously stored identity, and React effect replay cannot consume the same callback twice. The pending local-app destination survives completion.

Add exact-secret self-revocation for standard API credentials so local apps can disconnect browser-issued CLI credentials. Primary-storage tombstones support response-loss retries, inference revocation precedes the mutation, and the durable audit shares its transaction. Only DELETE on the current-credential leaf delegates authentication to that leaf; no caller-supplied key ID is accepted.

- Full monorepo verification is not yet qualified. Production backport and real login/logout acceptance are in progress; this draft does not claim deployed acceptance.
3f:T1065,Fixes #33123

# Relates to

The LIFE action's `query_calendar_today` operation (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/actions/life.ts`) asked for `dayRange(0)` with `timeZone: resolveDefaultTimeZone()`. `dayRange` (`lifeops/google/format-helpers.ts`) took the **host** clock's midnight (`setHours(0, 0, 0, 0)` in the process zone) plus a fixed 86,400,000 ms, so the owner's timezone fact was never consulted. Owner in New York asking "what's on my calendar today" at 21:00 on 2026-10-02 (`2026-10-03T01:00Z`), develop `811c3fac48`:

```
TZ=UTC              develop window: 2026-10-03T00:00Z .. 2026-10-04T00:00Z   (tomorrow)
TZ=Pacific/Auckland develop window: 2026-10-02T11:00Z .. 2026-10-03T11:00Z   (7 h off)
TZ=America/New_York develop window: 2026-10-02T04:00Z .. 2026-10-03T04:00Z   (only correct when host = owner zone)
owner's local day:                  2026-10-02T04:00Z .. 2026-10-03T04:00Z
```

DST days were also always 24 hours, so on a spring-forward day the query reached into 01:00 the next day.

# Change

- `dayRange(offset, timeZone, now)` returns local midnight to the next local midnight in `timeZone` (the plugin's existing `getZonedDateParts` / `addDaysToLocalDate` / `buildUtcDateFromLocalParts`), so DST days span 23 or 25 hours.
- The LIFE `query_calendar_today` path resolves the owner's zone with core `resolveCalendarTimeZone(runtime, now)` (the fail-closed resolver the plugin registers for day boundaries); an invalid or unreadable owner zone returns `success: false` with `CALENDAR_TIME_ZONE_INVALID`/`CALENDAR_TIME_ZONE_UNAVAILABLE` and no feed query and uses it for both the window and the feed `timeZone`.
- `weekRange`/`futureRange` have no callers and are unchanged.

40:T1323,Fixes #33145

# Relates to

`PERSONAL_ASSISTANT action=sign_document` (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/actions/owner-surfaces.ts`) queued a `sign_document` approval whose `documentId` was `signature-<messageId>`, a name no DocumentRequest ever had. RESOLVE_REQUEST dispatches an approved `sign_document` row by looking that DocumentRequest up (`resolve-request.ts`, `getDocumentRequest`), so every approval from this surface ended in:

```
success: false, error: DOCUMENT_REQUEST_NOT_FOUND
"…DocumentRequest signature-<id> no longer exists (the document store does not survive restarts) — nothing was dispatched. Please re-issue the signature request."
```

The message blames a restart, but the request never existed. The seed turns of the live-only scenarios that use this surface (`document-signature-chase`, `nda-counterparty-redline-handoff`, `board-consent-signature-emergency`, and others) therefore cannot reach the `expectApprovalResolvedApproved` outcome they assert. The handler also stored the literal `"pending-signature-url"` when no URL was given.

`OWNER_DOCUMENTS subaction=request_signature` already does this correctly: it creates the DocumentRequest, queues the approval against its id, and schedules the deadline watcher.

# Change

- `document.ts`: the body of `handleRequestSignature` becomes `createSignatureRequest` (DocumentRequest + approval + deadline watcher + obligation ledger), exported to other actions as `enqueueSignatureRequest`. The OWNER_DOCUMENTS behaviour is unchanged. `requesteeEntityId` is optional in the helper, as it already is on `DocumentRequest`.
- `owner-surfaces.ts`: `sign_document` calls that helper with its existing title, deadline, URL and reason. This surface does not resolve a counterparty, so the request names none (the ledger records `counterparty: null`), and a missing URL stays `""`, as OWNER_DOCUMENTS already does. The result now includes `documentRequestId`.
- Behaviour change on this surface: the approval now expires at the signing deadline (default 48 h, as before) instead of a fixed 24 h, and a deadline watcher is scheduled, as for OWNER_DOCUMENTS requests.

41:T1cf4,# Relates to

Fixes #33172 (Reminder dispatch and fallback bodies use the host timezone instead of the owner's).

Same defect family as #33155 (cadence summaries in `actions/life.ts`), different file and path: this PR covers only the reminder delivery renderers in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/domains/reminders-service.ts`, as that issue's scoping note requires.

# Change

`dispatchReminderAttempt` already resolves and carries `timezone: string`, and the exact in-app one-time path passes it into `buildReminderBody`, but two owner-visible renderers dropped it and fell back to the host clock:

- `buildReminderDispatchPrompt` formatted `- due:` with `new Date(args.reminderAt).toLocaleString()` — no `timeZone`. With a UTC host and an `America/Los_Angeles` owner, a 4:00 PM PDT reminder was described to the delivery model as "11:00 PM", so the model anchored owner-facing copy to the server's wall clock.
- `renderReminderBody` called `buildReminderBody` without the `timezone` that function already supports, so the deterministic fallback body (what owners actually receive when `TEXT_SMALL` is unavailable, returns unusable text, or throws) rendered "Due: …" host-local too.

- `buildReminderDispatchPrompt` gains an optional `timezone` argument; when present, the due line renders `toLocaleString("en-US", { timeZone })`.
- `renderReminderBody` gains the same optional argument and threads it into both the fallback body and the dispatch prompt.
- The `dispatchReminderAttempt → renderReminderBody` call site passes `timezone: args.timezone` through.
- Callers that omit a timezone keep today's host-local rendering; no other caller of either function exists (checked across the repository; `LifeOpsService.renderReminderBody` forwards arguments verbatim).

No message shapes, statuses, or scheduling semantics change — only which wall clock the delivered copy is anchored to.

# Testing

- New focused suite `reminders-service.dispatch-timezone.test.ts` — 6 tests pass under host `TZ=UTC` and again under host `TZ=America/New_York` (invariance):
  - `buildReminderDispatchPrompt` renders `- due: 8/20/2026, 4:00:00 PM` (owner PDT) identically under both host zones, and keeps `11:00:00 PM` host-local rendering when no timezone is supplied.
  - `buildReminderBody` renders `Due: 8/20/2026, 4:00:00 PM` under both host zones; host-local rendering preserved when omitted.
  - `renderReminderBody` (real `RemindersDomain`, no `useModel` → deterministic fallback) returns the owner-timezone body.
  - `dispatchReminderAttempt` delivers the in-app reminder with `deliveryMetadata.message` containing `Due: 8/20/2026, 4:00:00 PM` and not the host-local `11:00:00 PM`.
- Adjacent reminder/prompt suites pass: `reminders-service.checkin-chips`, `reminders-service.in-app-nudge`, `reminders-service.laddered-body`, `reminders-service.state-log-rollover`, `lifeops-llm-consumer-robustness`, `brief-optimized-prompt` — 6 files, 31 tests.
- `bun run typecheck` (plugins/plugin-personal-assistant) — passes.
- Biome check on both changed files — clean.
- Full plugin lane (`bun run test`): 334 files pass; 2 pre-existing failures (`src/routes/lifeops-calendar-route-ownership.test.ts`, `src/routes/lifeops-imessage-status-route.test.ts`, 9 tests) reproduce identically on pristine `origin/develop` at `3248f87109353350b3721127bfaebaf8f081a740` and are untouched by this change.
- Branch head tree verified byte-identical between the local commit and the published fork branch (`bdfe4e5f3cdb9f9e7b1797b12bf475af56e1a588`).

43:T741,## Problem and change

Detached resident shutdown and launcher startup currently signal every process matching the shared Bun/bundle path, which can terminate admitted workflow workers. Replace broad path-based signals with resident argument and process-identity validation, and remove the launcher's `pkill` calls.

A refused or uncertain stop now preserves the in-memory ownership, credentials, launch stamp, and output pumps. The detached stop runs before cleanup under the lifecycle lock so it can be retried without racing adoption.

This ports Alpha Phone's `android-resident-exact-stop.patch` and `android-resident-launcher-no-kill.patch`, plus the ownership-retention correction found during review. It is stacked on #33026 because it extends that PR's survivor inventory. Retarget to develop after the dependency lands.

## Validation

- Existing staging suite: 26/26 passed. The real shell launch setup now fails on any `pkill` invocation.
- Biome check of both changed TypeScript files passed; `git diff --check` passed.
- Added an opt-in Android fixture exercising two rejected stops through the real service method with a deliberately absent deployment. It verifies ownership, credentials, token file, and pumps remain intact; validation fails before process enumeration/signaling.
- Production Android app and instrumentation compilation passed offline (515 tasks, 4m54s). Cached Robolectric execution has not been completed and is not claimed.

## Remaining boundaries

This uses immediate identity revalidation followed by PID signaling, not an atomic pidfd operation. Real Android resident restart/worker survival and PID reuse behavior are unverified. No crash investigation, SIGSYS diagnostics, emulator access, or live resident signaling was performed. Full TypeScript package/root verification remains outside this scoped offline run.

44:Tbe1,# Change

The dock host could navigate into a browser without embedding opt-in, and presenting an already embedded browser could select its launcher tab. This adds installed certificate preflight for all four Chromium route components and preserves an existing embedded pane during presentation.

`setBrowserDockVisible({visible})` resizes the existing host-owned split without launching or reloading a URL. It requires Window extension 3+, an in-process opened session and exactly one current split with this host as primary. Missing, ambiguous or recreated sessions reject. The response is a request receipt, not page or task authority.

45:T1a42,# Relates to

No tracked issue. Found during a queue-cleared audit: on a source checkout whose free space is below the llama.cpp submodule footprint, `bun install` aborts entirely with an opaque git error after downloading the pack twice (git retries a failed submodule clone once), and the failed writes leave partial pack data behind. Reproduced three times on 2026-10-03 (transcript below); the same host completed every other install step.

# Change

`ensure-fused-inference-install.ts` now probes the filesystem holding the repository before the first llama.cpp submodule clone and refuses with a typed, actionable error when free space is below `FUSED_INFERENCE_MIN_FREE_BYTES` (3 GB, covering the history clone plus native build staging). Behavior:

- The probe covers only the clone case; an already-initialized submodule updates in place and is never probed.
- The probe fails open: on platforms without `statfs` (Windows), missing repo path, or any probe error, install proceeds exactly as before.
- The refusal message names the measured free space, the requirement, the submodule path, and the documented `ELIZA_SKIP_FUSED_INFERENCE_SETUP=1` opt-out, so constrained hosts keep a working non-fused path.
- No new environment variables and no install semantics change: the failure mode moves from "minutes of doomed downloads, then `git exited 1`" to "immediate typed message before any bytes move".

Material risk: a host with less than 3 GB free that would have completed a lighter clone is now refused up front. Such a host still has the documented skip flag, and the native build stage that follows the clone needs scratch space anyway.

46:T1cf0,# Relates to

#33002 (durable approved workflows and device actions with managed Cloud) added hosted digests, but none of their routes can be reached through the agent's HTTP dispatcher. This was the blocking point of [this review](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/33002#pullrequestreview-5383662522), and it is unchanged on develop `811c3fac48`:

- `plugins/plugin-workflow/src/routes/workflow-routes.ts:147-205` serves nine method/path pairs under `/api/workflow/hosted/` (live calendars and accounts, sources, source revoke, loops, results and ack).
- `GET /api/workflow/status` advertises them with `hostedDigestProtocol: 1` (`:237`).
- `plugins/plugin-workflow/src/plugin-routes.ts` is the plugin's only route table, and it has no `hosted` entry. Plugin routes only reach a handler through a registered pattern (`packages/agent/src/api/plugin-route-path.ts`, used by every dispatcher: `runtime-plugin-routes.ts`, `dispatch-route.ts`, `hono-mount.ts`, `server-lazy-routes.ts`).

**Result:** every hosted digest request answers 404 even though `/status` tells clients the protocol is available. `hosted-digests-http.test.ts` and `hosted-live-google-http.test.ts` call `handleWorkflowRoutes` from a bare `http.createServer`, which bypasses the route table, so they never saw this.

# Change

- The nine pairs are registered in `plugin-routes.ts` against the existing `workflowHandler`, as `rawPath` routes like the rest of the table. They are not `public`, so they go through the same auth gate as the other workflow routes, and the handler's owner scoping is unchanged.
- **Body caps are kept:**
  - The dispatcher parses JSON bodies (default 1 MiB) before the handler runs, and `readBody` then returns that pre-parsed body, so the handler's own `readBody` limits would no longer apply.
  - `POST …/hosted/live-calendars` and `POST …/hosted/sources` therefore restate their 2000- and 20000-byte caps as `maxBodyBytes`.
  - The other hosted POSTs (`sources/revoke`, `loops`, `results/ack`) keep the dispatcher's 1 MiB default. That is already stricter than their handler limit (`MAX_WORKFLOW_JSON_BYTES`, 2 MB), and restating 2 MB would loosen them. The route comment says so, and a test pins it.

`/api/workflow/hosted/cloud-delegation/` stays app-managed (`route-auth-policy.ts`) and is not touched.

47:T11de,# Relates to

`POST /api/v1/earnings/payout/stripe-connect/transfer` debits an affiliate payout with `debitAffiliatePayout` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/affiliate-payouts.ts`), which calls `redeemableEarningsService.reduceEarnings`. That function exists for refund reconciliation, where earnings really do shrink: it lowers `total_earned`, `available_balance` and `earned_from_affiliates`, and never touches `total_redeemed`. The schema documents `total_earned` and `total_redeemed` as only ever increasing, with `available = earned - redeemed - pending`.

So after a $40 payout on $100 of affiliate earnings, `/api/v1/credits/summary` and `/api/v1/redemptions/balance` show `totalEarned: 60` and `totalRedeemed: 0`, and the payout disappears from the dashboard. When Stripe rejects the transfer, the route restores the balance with `addEarnings`, which books the restore as new affiliate income.

# Change

- `reduceEarnings` gains a `countAsRedeemed` mode: `available_balance -= amount` and `total_redeemed += amount`, leaving `total_earned` and the per-source column alone. `debitAffiliatePayout` uses it.
- `addEarnings` gains a `reversesRedemption` mode: `available_balance += amount` and `total_redeemed -= amount`. The rejected-transfer restore in the route uses it.
- The ledger rows (`adjustment`/`affiliate` with the payout key, and `earning` with `:refund`), the idempotency lookups and the payable cap are unchanged. Every other caller keeps the default behavior.

# Testing

New `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/affiliate-payout-totals.pglite.test.ts` runs the real ledger service on PGlite with migration 0500 applied. It credits $100 of affiliate earnings, pays out $40 with `debitAffiliatePayout`, then restores it the way the route does on a rejected transfer.

- On develop: `Expected: 100, Received: 60` (`totalEarned`).
- With this change: available 60, earned 100, redeemed 40, `earned_from_affiliates` 100; after the restore, available 100, earned 100, redeemed 0.
- These suites pass: `redeemable-earnings-fail-closed` (3), `creator-monetization-retirement` (7), `container-billing-idempotency` (18), `stripe-event` (48), `stripe-event-waifu` (5).
- `tsc --noEmit` and `biome check` report nothing for the changed files.

49:T18d7,# Relates to

Follow-up to the review contract established on #33035 (maintainer review): invalid browser-wallet request input is a client error and must be answered `400`, never the `503` signer-unavailable status that invites clients to retry a payload that can never be valid. #33035 fixed this for `/api/wallet/browser-solana-transaction` (cluster and `transactionBase64` validation); the sibling `POST /api/wallet/browser-solana-sign-message` route was missed and still had the defect this PR fixes.

# Change

`POST /api/wallet/browser-solana-sign-message` with a missing or blank `message`/`messageBase64` threw a plain `Error("message or messageBase64 is required.")` from `resolveBrowserSolanaMessageBytes`, which the route's catch translated to **503** — telling the dApp the Solana signer was temporarily unavailable. Realistic caller impact: a dApp that sends a malformed sign-message request sees "unavailable, retry later" and retries an unusable payload instead of surfacing a fixable input error to its user.

- `resolveBrowserSolanaMessageBytes` now throws the route file's existing `BrowserWalletInputError` type (same class the transaction route uses; its docblock is extended to cover the missing sign-message payload).
- The route's catch maps `BrowserWalletInputError` to `400` and keeps `503` for signer/key/network failures, mirroring the transaction route's merged pattern verbatim.
- The EVM sign-message route already validates before its try (400) and is untouched.

No request/response field shapes change; only the status code and error type for this one invalid-input path.

# Testing

- `bun run ../../node_modules/vitest/vitest.mjs run --config vitest.config.ts src/api/` (plugins/plugin-wallet) — 5 files, 15 tests pass, including the new `browser-solana-sign-message.test.ts` which exercises the real route handler: missing/blank payloads answer exactly `400` with `message or messageBase64 is required.`; a present `message` and a `messageBase64` payload sign with `200`; a missing `SOLANA_PRIVATE_KEY` keeps the `503` unavailable contract.
- Discrimination check: with the fix reverted, the missing-payload test fails (route answers 503); restored, it passes.
- `bun run typecheck` (plugins/plugin-wallet) — passes.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"zai-coding-plan","model":"glm-5.3-flash","client":"opencode","skill_revision":"N/A - ordinary GitHub contribution without optional run evidence"} -->4a:T18d7,# Relates to

Follow-up to the review contract established on #33035 (maintainer review): invalid browser-wallet request input is a client error and must be answered `400`, never the `503` signer-unavailable status that invites clients to retry a payload that can never be valid. #33035 fixed this for `/api/wallet/browser-solana-transaction` (cluster and `transactionBase64` validation); the sibling `POST /api/wallet/browser-solana-sign-message` route was missed and still had the defect this PR fixes.

# Change

`POST /api/wallet/browser-solana-sign-message` with a missing or blank `message`/`messageBase64` threw a plain `Error("message or messageBase64 is required.")` from `resolveBrowserSolanaMessageBytes`, which the route's catch translated to **503** — telling the dApp the Solana signer was temporarily unavailable. Realistic caller impact: a dApp that sends a malformed sign-message request sees "unavailable, retry later" and retries an unusable payload instead of surfacing a fixable input error to its user.

- `resolveBrowserSolanaMessageBytes` now throws the route file's existing `BrowserWalletInputError` type (same class the transaction route uses; its docblock is extended to cover the missing sign-message payload).
- The route's catch maps `BrowserWalletInputError` to `400` and keeps `503` for signer/key/network failures, mirroring the transaction route's merged pattern verbatim.
- The EVM sign-message route already validates before its try (400) and is untouched.

No request/response field shapes change; only the status code and error type for this one invalid-input path.

- Discrimination check: with the fix reverted, the missing-payload test fails (route answers 503); restored, it passes.

4b:T8f7,## Problem and change

Android native stream callers cannot cancel a socket while it is connecting or reading, and a socket EOF currently looks like successful completion. Add a cancellable native stream handle, preserve the existing two-argument entrypoint, and report EOF without a terminal frame as an unknown outcome. Retries remain connection-only; dispatched requests are never replayed.

Authenticate the kernel-reported socket peer UID before writing request bytes, including the buffered connection path and readiness probe. This ports Alpha Phone's `android-stream-cancellation.patch` onto develop without importing product UI or identity.

Add an opt-in native socket instrumentation fixture covering fragmented frames, explicit completion/error, premature EOF, cancellation during a read and retry sleep, and exactly one connection/POST after dispatch. Document how native callers use the handle and the fixture admission requirements.

## Validation

- Existing real HTTP/Vite stream integration suite: 6/6 passed, including browser cancellation closing the upstream connection. This is adjacent HTTP transport evidence, not execution of the changed Android implementation.
- Production app and instrumentation Java compilation passed offline: `:app:compileDebugJavaWithJavac :app:compileDebugAndroidTestJavaWithJavac` (515 tasks, 6m17s). Gradle 9.5.0, Java 21, Node 24.15.0; supported `ELIZA_ANDROID_SKIP_FORK_LLAMA_LIB=1` skips inference JNI staging but preserves real Java sources/dependencies.
- `git diff --check` passed.
- No dependencies installed; no emulator accessed.

## Remaining qualification / scope

- The new instrumentation fixture still requires on-device execution; Java compilation does not prove socket behavior or APK/native-runtime acceptance.
- This exposes native cancellation; existing upstream AgentPlugin still uses the two-argument method. Wiring renderer abort through Capacitor requires a follow-up and is not claimed here.
- Rejected foreign-UID socket peers require a separate Android process/UID qualification; the same-UID fixture does not prove that rejection path.
- Full TypeScript package/root verification was not run in the constrained scoped-dependency checkout. This remains a draft pending central review and hosted/native qualification.

Those PRs cover the disconnect route and ten optional-body mutation routes, but `POST /api/internal/discord/gateway/shutdown` was left on the old contract: its schema is fully optional (`pod_name?`), so a truncated non-empty body collapsed via `c.req.json().catch(() => ({}))` into the defaults and **released the caller's pod assignments** instead of failing. No dedicated issue exists for this route; no open PR touches it (verified against the live queue before branching).

# Change

Same boundary pattern as PR #33090 / #33121, with the `// error-policy:J3` untrusted-input comment.

4e:T10b1,Fixes #33113

# Relates to

`getLogs` in `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` filters only by the optional `entityId`, `roomId`, and `type`, and the `logs` table has no agent column, so in a database shared by several agents any call without a room filter returns every agent's logs. `deleteLogs` and `updateLogs` act on any id. Real `PgliteDatabaseAdapter` on develop `811c3fac48`, agents A and B in one database, one `inference_timing` log each:

```
A.getLogs({type:'inference_timing'}) -> [{"who":"agentB"},{"who":"agentA"}]
A.getLogs({limit:MAX})               -> [{"who":"agentB"},{"who":"agentA"}]
```

Production callers that pass no room filter:

- `packages/agent/src/services/agent-export.ts` (`getLogs({ limit: 500, offset })`): agent A's export bundle includes agent B's logs.
- `packages/agent/src/runtime/logs-retention-service.ts` (`getLogs({ limit: MAX_SAFE_INTEGER })` then `deleteLogs`): A's retention policy is applied to, and deletes, B's rows.
- `plugins/plugin-assistant/src/services/message/inference-timing.ts` (`getLogs({ type, limit })` then `deleteLogs`): keeps the newest N `inference_timing` rows across all agents and deletes another agent's rows.

# Change

- `getLogs` joins the owning room and requires `rooms.agent_id = this.agentId`, the same scoping `getAgentRunSummaries` already applies to this table, and orders by `(created_at desc, id desc)` so export's limit/offset pages are stable when timestamps tie.
- `deleteLogs` and `updateLogs` only touch rows whose room belongs to this agent (`room_id IN (SELECT id FROM rooms WHERE agent_id = $agent)`).
- Single-agent databases see no change.

4f:Tccd,# Relates to

Fixes #33189

## Summary

`disconnectManagedGoogleConnection` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/agent-google-connector/shared.ts:547`) falls through to `connections[0]` when an **explicit** `connectionId` matches nothing in the caller's scoped connections — a stale, wrong, or cross-side UUID gets **HTTP 200 `{ok:true}` while a different connection (the first listed) is revoked**.

The fix splits the two contracts the route serves:

- **Explicit `connectionId`** → `find` by id; no match → `fail(404, "Google connection not found.")` — the same error the file's `getConnectionStatus` (`:479`) already uses for explicit grants — with **zero revoke calls**. This also replaces the silent no-op when no scoped connections exist (explicit id + empty list now 404s).
- **Omitted/null `connectionId`** → unchanged disconnect-active-or-first behavior, including the no-op on an empty list.

The route (`v1/eliza/google/disconnect`) already maps `AgentGoogleConnectorError` to its status — the 404 propagates with no route changes.

## Verification (real monorepo: bun 1.4.2, from `packages/cloud/shared`)

New `disconnect.test.ts` — the REAL function with `managedGoogleConnectorDeps.oauthService` spied (sibling `gmail.test.ts` pattern), revoke call ledger:

```
head:  5 pass / 0 fail
  (pass) 404s on an explicit-but-unknown id instead of revoking the first connection
  (pass) 404s on an explicit id when no scoped connections exist
  (pass) revokes exactly the named connection for a known explicit id
  (pass) keeps the omitted-id active-or-first contract
  (pass) keeps the no-op for an omitted id with no connections
```

**Reverse verification** (function reverted to `develop`, test unchanged): **3 pass / 2 fail** — precisely both explicit-id rows fail (defect live on develop: the unknown-id promise *resolves* and the first connection is revoked); the three unchanged-behavior rows stay green.

**Sibling regression:** `gmail.test.ts` + `gmail-attachments.test.ts` + new suite at head: **18 pass / 0 fail**. (The `inbox-provider.http.integration` failures in one-process full-directory runs are pre-existing local-server port contention — identical on develop, individually green.)

```

biome check (both files):               No fixes applied
```

# Change

`calendarEventOccursOn` now places a timed event on every viewer-local civil day the interval occupies. A local-midnight end is exclusive, matching all-day date math. Same-day timed events still land on the viewer's local start day. All-day exclusive-end placement is unchanged.

# Evidence Details

Reproduction on `develop` `811c3fac48df3c3e4e8e7414ef78e544fa7dac08`:

A timed interval `2026-06-16T02:00:00.000Z`–`2026-06-16T05:00:00.000Z` is 22:00–01:00 in `America/New_York`. `calendarEventOccursOn(..., "2026-06-16", "America/New_York")` returned `false` because only the start instant's civil day was used. `CalendarSection` and `SimpleCalendarView` filter the month/week grid with that helper, so the meeting was missing from 16 June.

After the change the same interval is true on both `2026-06-15` and `2026-06-16` in New York, and a 48-hour timed block (`2026-09-16T00:00Z`–`2026-09-18T00:00Z`) occupies 15–17 June in New York and 16–18 June in Auckland. All-day school closures remain 16–17 September exclusive of the 18th.

Duplication: no open PR touches `plugins/plugin-calendar/src/components/calendar/event-days.ts`. This is event-span placement in the calendar grid, not the LIFE/Google Fit "today" query-window work.

51:T1992,# Relates to

`POST /api/crypto/direct-payments/:id/confirm` calls `await c.req.json()` without a malformed-body guard. A truncated or non-JSON body throws `SyntaxError` inside the `try`, and `failureResponse` maps it to 500 `{success:false, code:"internal_error"}` because `inferStatusFromLegacyError` has no 4xx heuristic for JSON syntax messages. A client syntax error is therefore reported as a server fault on a route that credits an organization on success, polluting 5xx alerting and inviting retry-on-500 of a body that can never succeed. This is the same class already fixed on the sibling `attach-tx` route in this directory (`await c.req.json().catch(() => null)`) and in 11 routes via #33121, plus #33090 / #33051 — this route was not among them. No existing issue or open PR covers this file (verified via live-report, `gh search`, and open-PR file scan).

# Change

One-line guard mirroring the sibling `attach-tx` route in the same directory:

- `const body = await c.req.json()` becomes `await c.req.json().catch(() => null)` with an `// error-policy:J3` comment, so a malformed body flows into the existing `confirmSchema.safeParse` rejection and returns the route's established 400 `{error:"Invalid request", details}` contract.
- No schema, auth, payment-lookup, hash-format, service-call, or success-response change. Valid bodies behave exactly as before.

Material risk: low. The change only converts a 500 into the route's own 400 for bodies that previously threw before validation; every previously valid body parses identically and reaches the same service call.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"opencode","model":"muse-spark-1.3-contributor-free","client":"opencode","skill_revision":"N/A - ordinary GitHub contribution without optional run evidence"} -->52:Tf2c,Fixes #33204

# Relates to

`GenerateTextParams.signal` is the runtime's cancellation for a turn. The elizaOS Cloud streaming text path and the embeddings path forward it to `requestRaw`. The buffered (non-streaming) text paths in `plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/models/text.ts` did not:

- `generateNativeChatCompletion` (`POST /chat/completions`) and the `/responses` path called `requestRaw` with `{ headers, json, timeoutMs }` only.
- `requestNativeWithWarmingRetry` called `sleepMs(delayMs)` without the signal, even though `sleepMs` accepts one.

A stopped turn's buffered call (for example a tool-planning or evaluator call) therefore kept running until `resolveTextTimeoutMs()`. On a warming gateway it also sat through the whole 503 backoff ladder. All the while it held a `withNativeChatLimit` permit, which gates the shared key for every concurrent native call. The cloud SDK's `requestRaw` merges a caller `signal` with `timeoutMs` (`packages/cloud/sdk/src/http.ts`). The comment claiming it "honors only a single signal" was out of date, and this PR removes it.

# Change

- Both buffered `requestRaw` calls pass `signal: params.signal` when the runtime supplied one.
- `requestNativeWithWarmingRetry` takes that signal and passes it to the backoff `sleepMs`, so a cancel during warming ends the wait without another request.
- The timeout and the streaming path are unchanged.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop`.
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (comments).

- `__tests__/unit/text-warming-retry.test.ts`, two new cases:
  - "aborts an in-flight buffered completion with the caller's signal": fetch settles only on abort. On develop the completion is still pending after the cancel; here it rejects with the cancel reason.
  - "ends a buffered completion's warming backoff on caller abort without another request": on develop it ran the full ladder and ended in `ElizaCloudGatewayWarmingExhaustedError`; here it rejects with the cancel reason after one request.
  - The file is 17/17.
- Whole package `bunx vitest run` in `plugins/plugin-elizacloud`: the same 9 files fail to load or fail on develop in this checkout (an older, borrowed install). With the fix, 721 tests pass (the develop baseline is 719 plus these 2).
- `tsc` reports nothing in the touched files; its 5 errors are missing `packages/ui` dependencies in this checkout. Biome is clean.
