53:Tf9c,Fixes #33118

# Relates to

The `quiet_hours` gate (`plugins/plugin-scheduling/src/scheduled-task/gate-registry.ts`) computes the time until the window ends by subtracting minute-of-day values and returns `until: { offsetMinutes }`; the runner adds that as elapsed time (`runner.ts`). A DST change inside the window moves the wall clock, so the deferred task refires at the wrong local time. Quiet hours 22:00–07:00, real gate on develop `811c3fac48` (refire = now + offset):

```
develop America/New_York     now 3/7, 22:30 EST          -> refire 3/8, 08:00 EDT
develop America/New_York     now 10/31, 22:30 EDT        -> refire 11/1, 06:00 EST   (still quiet; defers again)
develop Europe/London        now 3/28, 22:30 GMT         -> refire 3/29, 08:00 GMT+1
develop Australia/Lord_Howe  now 10/3, 22:30 GMT+10:30   -> refire 10/4, 07:30 GMT+11
fix     (all four)                                        -> refire 07:00 local
```

Production callers: the quota-progress check-in (`plugin-personal-assistant` `reminders-service.ts`, `{ kind: "quiet_hours", params: { highPriorityBypass: false } }`) and the unanswered-question follow-up (`unanswered-question-followup.ts`), with `ownerFacts.quietHours` from the owner fact store.

# Change

- The gate resolves the window's end with the existing `resolveLocalHHMMToIso` in the zone it used to decide "now is quiet" (the quiet-hours zone, else the owner's zone, else UTC; made explicit so the window test and the end instant cannot disagree), on the next local day when the window wraps midnight and has already started, and returns `until: { atIso }`, which the runner already supports.
- The previous minute-count path remains only as a guard if the end instant does not resolve after now.

54:T1548,# Relates to

Fixes #33208.

The shipped `STEER_LIQUIDITY` wallet path treated every Steer vault with a
`poolAddress` as belonging to an explicitly requested token address. An
unknown address could then trigger the protocol-wide fallback and return every
vault on the chain.

# Change

- Require an exact, case-insensitive `token0` or `token1` address match for
  explicit token-address queries.
- Do not broaden an unmatched explicit address to all Steer vaults.
- Preserve the existing all-vault fallback for symbol/general searches.
- Add regression coverage through the public production service method for a
  matching address and an unknown address.

The behavior change is deliberately limited to explicit EVM token addresses.

# Testing

- `bun install` — passed with pinned Bun 1.4.2/Node 24.15.0; 74/74 workspace
  builds and 20 plugin views completed.
- Focused regression test — passed, 1/1.

  skipped; 448 tests passed, 1 skipped.
- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-wallet typecheck` — passed.
- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-wallet lint:check` — passed with 13 existing
  advisory warnings outside this change.
- `bun run verify` — passed on the committed revision: 284/284 Turbo tasks and
  all closing repository audits.
- Pre-fix and post-fix reproduction invoked the actual
  `SteerLiquidityService.getTokenLiquidityStats()` method. Only the external
  Steer SDK response and vault-enrichment boundary were deterministic.

55:Tf29,Fixes #33207

# Relates to

The `KNOWLEDGE_GRAPH` action's `set_relationship` op (`plugins/plugin-relationships/src/actions/entity.ts`) is documented to "upsert a typed edge". It called `RelationshipStore.upsert` without a `relationshipId`. The store keys upserts on that id and generates a fresh `rel_<uuid>` when it's absent, so `ON CONFLICT (relationship_id)` (and the record-store path, also keyed by id) never matched. The `(from, to, type)` index is not unique.

On develop `7d8732d878`, on a real PGlite runtime: the owner says "Pat is my manager" twice, so `set_relationship { toEntityId, relationshipType: "manages" }` runs twice. That leaves two active `self -[manages]-> Pat` edges with different ids. `GET /api/lifeops/relationships` and the relationships viewer list both.

# Change

`set_relationship` first looks up the active edge with the same `(fromEntityId, toEntityId, type)` through `RelationshipStore.list`:

- **If one exists:** it upserts that edge by its id. `state`, `metadata` and `createdAt` are kept, the new evidence is merged into the existing evidence, and `source`/`confidence` are set as before.
- **Otherwise:** it creates the edge exactly as today.

Retired edges are left alone, as the default `list` filter excludes them. I chose this over `observe()`, which also bumps `interactionCount`/`lastInteractionAt`: stating a relationship is not an interaction.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (`7d8732d878`).
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (call-site comment).

- `test/relationships.real-db.test.ts` (real PGlite `AgentRuntime`, the registered action): new case "set_relationship updates the existing edge instead of adding a duplicate" runs the action twice and expects one edge with evidence `["first chat", "second chat"]`. It fails on develop's `entity.ts` and passes here.
- `test/entity-action.test.ts`: new case "restating an active edge updates it in place" (upsert by `rel_1` with merged evidence). The store mock's `list` now filters by `from`/`to`/`type` like the real store, so an unrelated fixture edge isn't reused.
- Whole package `bunx vitest run` in `plugins/plugin-relationships`: 116 passed; 6 fail in `src/knowledge-graph/record-store.test.ts`, identically on develop in this checkout. `bun run typecheck` clean; Biome clean.

56:Tce4,## Summary
- `runtime.getMemoriesByWorldId` passes `worldId`. SQL finds that world's rooms and reads their messages.
- SQLite read `worldIds` only, so a runtime call matched every memory, and `limit: 0` was treated as unlimited.
- SQLite now resolves `worldId` or `worldIds` through rooms, defaults the table to messages, and treats `limit: 0` as an empty page.

## Test plan
- [x] `cd plugins/plugin-sqlite && bunx vitest run --config vitest.config.ts adapter.test.ts -t \"returns messages from the requested world\"` — 1 passed
- [ ] `bun run verify` — not run. `bun install` stops in the llama.cpp postinstall because `xcrun` cannot find metal.

# Relates to

SQLite `getMemoriesByWorldId({ worldId })` returned every memory, including other worlds and non-message tables.

# Change

The lookup accepts the runtime `worldId` and the adapter `worldIds`, keeps messages in those worlds' rooms, and honors a zero limit.

# Testing

59:Tebd,Fixes #33202

# Relates to

`GenerateTextParams.signal` (`packages/core/src/types/model.ts`) is filled in by the runtime, and "HTTP-based providers pass it into fetch". The model dispatcher sets it from the turn's streaming context, so a user stopping a turn aborts it. The OpenAI handler forwards it as `abortSignal`, and the elizaOS Cloud stream path forwards it too.

The Anthropic text handler (`plugins/plugin-anthropic/models/text.ts`) built `generateParams` without `abortSignal`. The signal stayed in the normalized params and was never read. A stopped turn's `generateText`/`streamText` request therefore kept generating, and was billed, up to `maxOutputTokens` with nobody reading the result.

`executeWithRetry` (`utils/retry.ts`) also lists `AbortError` as retryable. As soon as the signal is honoured, a cancelled `generateText` would be re-sent three more times with 500/1000/2000 ms sleeps before the cancellation surfaced. On develop `7d8732d878`, a cancelled non-streaming request through `handleTextSmall` made 4 provider calls in about 3.6 s.

# Change

- `generateParams` carries `abortSignal: params.signal` when the runtime supplied one, for both `generateText` and `streamText`.
- `executeWithRetry` takes the caller's signal as an optional fourth argument and rethrows without retrying once it is aborted. Other `AbortError`s (for example SDK timeouts) keep today's retry behaviour. The image handler's call is unchanged.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop` (`7d8732d878`).
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (parameter doc and comments).

- `native-plumbing.shape.test.ts`: new case "forwards the runtime cancellation signal and does not retry a cancelled request". `handleTextSmall` with `signal` must call `generateText` once, with `abortSignal` set to that signal. On develop: `expected "vi.fn()" to be called 1 times, but got 4 times`.
- `error-policy.shape.test.ts`: new case "does not retry once the caller's signal is aborted". On develop's `retry.ts`: `got 3 times`.
- Whole package `bunx vitest run` in `plugins/plugin-anthropic`: 15/15 files, 149 tests. `bun run typecheck` clean; Biome clean.

5a:Tdf3,## Problem

Four code-fence patterns in the model-output parsing path spell the info string `(?:json|json5)?`. Everything after that group (`\s*\r?\n?`) can match empty, so on a ```` ```json5 ```` fence the regex engine takes the `json` branch, the overall match still succeeds, and the captured body starts with the leftover `5` (e.g. `5\n{"a": 1}`). Every downstream parser then rejects it:

| Site | Effect of a ```` ```json5 ```` fenced response |
|---|---|
| `packages/core/src/text/model-output.ts` (`jsonBlockPattern`) | `parseJSONObjectFromText` returns `null`; `extractAndParseJSONObjectFromText` throws |
| `plugins/plugin-calendar/src/internal/detail.ts` (`MODEL_CODE_FENCE_PATTERN`) | `parseCalendarJsonRecord` returns `null` |
| `plugins/plugin-inbox/src/inbox/priority-scoring.ts` (`STRUCTURED_CODE_FENCE_PATTERN`) | the whole scoring batch fails ("did not return JSON scores or a legacy JSON array") |
| `plugins/plugin-inbox/src/inbox/triage-classifier.ts` (`TRIAGE_CODE_FENCE_PATTERN`) | legacy array triage throws "did not return a JSON array" |

The shared helper `unwrapWholeCodeFence` (`packages/core/src/utils/code-fence.ts`) already handles this correctly by trying labels longest-first.

## Fix

List the longer label first — `(?:json5|json)?` — at all four sites, and drop the duplicated `json|json` alternative in `priority-scoring.ts`. `plugins/plugin-assistant/src/runtime/evaluator.ts:2066` uses the same ordering but is left unchanged: it only parses the trailing object, so a leftover `5` stays in the preceding prose and is harmless there.

## Prior work

The JSON5-label defect was reported in #30536 (@attentionhead), which a maintainer confirmed as real and consolidated into #30875 (@hermesagent270-commits). #30875 was closed on 2026-09-26 because the package restructure moved the affected files, with a request to open a fresh PR against the current layout if still needed. This is that fresh PR against the current paths; credit for the diagnosis goes to both.

## Verification

- `packages/core/src/text/model-output.test.ts` (new): `json5` / JSON5-only syntax / uppercase `JSON5` / `json` / unlabeled fences parse to the object; a `json5`-fenced array is extracted without throwing.
- `plugins/plugin-calendar/test/detail.test.ts`: added a ```` ```json5 ```` case to `parseCalendarJsonRecord`.
- `plugins/plugin-inbox/src/inbox/priority-scoring.test.ts`: a ```` ```json5 ````-fenced `{"scores":[…]}` response is scored and `reportError` is not called.
- `plugins/plugin-inbox/test/triage-code-fence.test.ts` (new): a ```` ```json5 ````-fenced legacy array is classified by `classifyMessages`.
- Red→green: every new case fails on `develop` with the effects in the table above and passes with the fix; the `json` / unlabeled controls pass both ways.
- Full `plugin-inbox` suite: 296 passed. `plugin-calendar` full suite: identical results with and without this change (its pre-existing failures come from unbuilt workspace packages in my environment).
- `tsc --noEmit` (test files included) and Biome clean on the changed files.

5d:Tc5b,Fixes #33120

# Relates to

`queryEntities` in `plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` applies `limit`/`offset` with no `ORDER BY`, so Postgres returns rows in physical order. An `UPDATE` rewrites a row's physical position, so a caller paging across a concurrent write skips one entity and serves another twice. Real `PgliteDatabaseAdapter` on develop `811c3fac48`, four entities walked with `limit: 1` and an `updateEntity` on the first page's row:

```
walk with limit=1 across an updateEntity: ["e0","e2","e3","e0","<empty>"]
distinct served: 3   (e1 never served, e0 twice)
```

Production caller: `plugins/plugin-form/src/storage.ts` `getRestorableSessions` pages entities 100 at a time, so an entity write during the scan drops a live form session from restore. This is the sibling of the ordered room/relationship pagination fixed in #32024.

# Change

`queryEntities` orders by `(created_at, id)` before `limit`/`offset`. Explicit `entityIds` queries keep their input order (sorted afterwards, unchanged).

# Relates to

`GENERATE_MEDIA` (`plugins/plugin-assistant/src/features/advanced-capabilities/actions/generateMedia.ts`) builds its request from `quality`, `style`, `negativePrompt`, `instrumental`, `genre`, and `voice` (`buildRequest`), and the production media service forwards them to providers (`packages/agent/src/services/media-generation.ts`: `voice` to speech, `instrumental`/`genre` to music, `quality`/`style`/`negativePrompt` to images; the cloud music model reads `instrumental` and `genre`). None of the six is declared in the action's `parameters`, and core `validateToolArgs` rejects undeclared arguments before the handler runs (`execute-planned-tool-call.ts` returns a failed tool result), so a planner call can never set them. Real `validateToolArgs` on develop `811c3fac48`:

```
{"mediaType":"audio","audioKind":"music",…,"instrumental":true,"genre":"lofi hip hop"} -> errors ["Unexpected argument 'instrumental'","Unexpected argument 'genre'"]
{"mediaType":"audio","audioKind":"tts",…,"voice":"nova"}                            -> errors ["Unexpected argument 'voice'"]
{"mediaType":"image",…,"quality":"hd","style":"natural","negativePrompt":"blurry"}  -> errors ["Unexpected argument 'quality'","Unexpected argument 'style'","Unexpected argument 'negativePrompt'"]
```

The planner's tool schema (`additionalProperties: false`) did not offer them either, so a user could not pick a TTS voice, ask for instrumental music or a genre, or set image quality, style, or a negative prompt. These six were never declared, before or after the September restructure (checked the pre-move `packages/core` copy).

# Change

Declare `instrumental` (boolean), `genre`, and `voice` as optional parameters. Every path that handles their media kind forwards them: `TEXT_TO_SPEECH` gets `voice` on the cloud, own-key and no-service fallback paths, and `AUDIO` gets `instrumental`/`genre` on the cloud and own-key paths. `voice` is described as the TTS provider's voice ID; the Eliza Cloud TTS path takes ElevenLabs voice IDs and discards OpenAI names such as `nova`.

The image controls (`quality`, `style`, `negativePrompt`) stay undeclared. Only the own-key image branch forwards them. The default cloud path (`generateImageWithModel`) and the no-service fallback call `useModel(ModelType.IMAGE, { prompt, size, count })`, and core `ImageGenerationParams` has no field that could carry them. Declaring them would turn today's loud rejection into a success that ignored the request. Carrying them through `ImageGenerationParams` is a separate core change. No handler change.

61:T1268,## Summary

- Loading an older chat page used an exclusive timestamp (`before - 1`), so any other message created in the same millisecond as the oldest loaded turn was skipped and never came back.
- The history request now sends that message id with the timestamp. The server uses the existing memory keyset cursor, which keeps the other messages in that millisecond and still excludes the cursor row. Timestamp-only clients keep the previous bound.
- Proved with a real agent HTTP server and SQLite history: the timestamp-only page returns only the earlier turn, and the id-paired page returns the two siblings that share the cursor millisecond.

63:T1057,Fixes #33125

# Relates to

PostgreSQL jsonb rejects JSON.stringify's escape for a lone UTF-16 surrogate (`\ud83d`: half of a pair, typically a truncated emoji) just as it rejects `\u0000`. The shared sanitizer in `plugins/plugin-sql/src/sanitize-json.ts` handles NUL (strict writes reject it with `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_NUL`, lenient sanitization strips it) and even counts a lone surrogate's 6-byte escape, but lets it through to the bind. Real `PgliteDatabaseAdapter` on develop `811c3fac48`, text `"hi \ud83d"`:

```
createMemory lone-surrogate text      THROWS invalid input syntax for type json
createMemory lone-surrogate metadata  THROWS invalid input syntax for type json
log lone-surrogate                    THROWS Failed query: insert into "logs" … ::jsonb …
```

Lone surrogates reach these writes from JSON request bodies (`JSON.parse('"\\ud83d"')`) and from any client that truncates by UTF-16 code unit; the message save path (`runtime.createMemory`) and the model-call log both go through this sanitizer.

# Change

Lone surrogates follow the existing NUL policy:

- strict writes (`serializeJsonb`: memory content and metadata, legacy JSON strings, object keys) reject them with a typed `ElizaError` `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE` instead of PostgreSQL's raw failure, and the memory write leaves no partial row (as for NUL);
- lenient sanitization (`sanitizeJsonObject`: log bodies) replaces them with U+FFFD, as it already strips NUL.

Well-formed surrogate pairs are unchanged. Cache, component, entity, and room metadata writes do not use this sanitizer and are out of scope here (they fail the same way on NUL today).

- [x] Updated relevant documentation (module header and helper docs).

- `sanitize-json.test.ts`: strict rejection for content, metadata, keys, and legacy JSON strings; a well-formed emoji preserved; lenient U+FFFD replacement for values and keys.
- `memory.real.test.ts` (real PGlite): a lone-surrogate message joins the existing invalid-content table: create rejects with `SQL_JSON_UNSUPPORTED_SURROGATE` and leaves no row; update fails without changing the stored row.
- `log.real.test.ts` (real PGlite): a log body with a truncated emoji is stored and read back as `"done \ufffd"`.
- 3 files, 104 passed. Against develop's `sanitize-json.ts`: 5 of the new cases fail (raw PostgreSQL errors); the well-formed guard passes on both.

SpaceXAI / Grok 4.7 / Cursor, self-reported.

Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com)66:Te2b,Fixes #33192

# Relates to

`XService.buildXDirectMessageMemory` (`plugins/plugin-x/src/services/x.service.ts`) decided whether a DM was the account's own with `senderId === runtime.agentId`. `senderId` is an X numeric user id and `runtime.agentId` is the agent's UUID, so the comparison was never true. Every outbound DM the account sent became a memory from a synthetic `x:user:<own id>` entity with `metadata.fromBot: false`. The planner-facing message connector (`fetchConnectorMessages`, registered as the X `fetchMessages` hook) therefore showed the agent's own replies as written by another user.

`listRecentDirectMessages` already computes the right fact (`isInbound: event.sender_id !== ownUserId`). The builder only copied it into `metadata.x.isInbound`. The sibling `buildXPostMemory` compares against the account's own X id correctly.

On develop `7d8732d878`, two DMs (own `outbound`, alice's `inbound`) through `fetchConnectorMessages`:

```
outbound  entityId=uuid("x:user:current-user")  fromBot=false   (expected agentId / true)
inbound   entityId=uuid("x:user:alice")         fromBot=false
```

# Change

`buildXDirectMessageMemory` treats `message.isInbound === false` as the account's own DM: `entityId` is `runtime.agentId` and `fromBot` is `true`. Inbound DMs and messages without the flag are unchanged.

SpaceXAI / Grok 4.7 / Cursor, self-reported.

Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com)69:Tebd,# Relates to

Chromium rejects reusing a task binding epoch. A first readback after a live unknown result, or another explicit readback after ambiguity or lost binding acknowledgement, could reuse an already consumed epoch and fail permanently.

# Change

Reserve a durable recovery epoch before every authorized read-only reconciliation attempt. Unknown results keep their operation and paused state; subsequent explicit checks receive a fresh epoch. Resolved checks retain the existing additional fence before explicit Resume. No automatic retry or effect is introduced.

# Testing

Normal agent Vitest configuration: subprocess restart regression passes, including two explicit readbacks in one process. Restoring the foundation implementation makes the new retry case fail. Consumer native-protocol regression enforces Chromium strict increasing epochs and covers ambiguity and lost binding acknowledgements; it passes with the overlay. Consumer root verification passes 338 tests, typecheck and web build.

Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com)6d:Ta9f,## Summary

- SQLite `getMemories` treated `start`, `end`, and `createdAt` as missing whenever they were `0`. An epoch row leaked into every time window, and `end: 0` applied no upper bound at all.
- Bounds are now numeric checks, matching the SQL adapter: `0` is a real inclusive timestamp, and a missing `createdAt` stays outside a bounded query.
- Proved on a real SQLite adapter: a row at `createdAt: 0` is the only hit for `start: 0, end: 0`, and `start: 1, end: 10` returns only the later row.

Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com)6e:Tddc,Fixes #33194

# Relates to

`FormService.updateField` (`plugins/plugin-form/src/service.ts`) wrote an undo entry only when the field already had a value (`if (oldValue !== undefined)`). `startSession` creates fields without a default as `{ status: "empty" }`, so a field's first answer never reached `session.history`. `undoLastChange` pops the newest entry, which then belonged to an older change. Its branch that clears a field (`oldValue === undefined`) could never run.

The model's `formIntent: "undo"` reaches `undoLastChange` from the form extractor (`evaluators/extractor.ts`), and `ux.allowUndo` is on by default. On develop `ea01a4a828`, a form with `name` and `email`:

```
name=Bob, name=Rob, email=a@b.co, undo  ->  {field: "name", restoredValue: "Bob"}   email stays filled, name back to Bob
name=Bob, undo                          ->  null                                     nothing undone
```

# Change

- `updateField` always records the change. A first answer is recorded without `oldValue`, and `undoLastChange` already treats that as "clear the field".
- `FieldHistoryEntry.oldValue` becomes optional (an additive widening). `undoLastChange`'s `restoredValue` is `undefined` when it cleared a field. Its only caller reads `result.field`.
- `maxUndoSteps` trimming is unchanged.

# Testing

- [x] Targets `develop` and has no conflicts with the latest `origin/develop`.
- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (`FieldHistoryEntry` doc).

- `service-hardening.test.ts`: new case "undoes the most recent change, including a field's first answer" runs the sequence above on the real `FormService` and then undoes back to an empty form. It fails on develop's `service.ts` (`expected { field: 'name', restoredValue: 'Bob' } to deeply equal { field: 'email', … }`) and passes here.
- Whole package `bunx vitest run` in `plugins/plugin-form`: 12/12 files, 161 tests. `bun run typecheck` clean; Biome clean.

Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com)70:Taa0,## Summary

- SQL `searchMemories` applies no similarity floor when `match_threshold` is omitted or `0`. SQLite substituted `0.5`, so local recall (including the PII memory scan, which omits the threshold) dropped matches the Postgres path keeps.
- An omitted or zero threshold is now no floor. A positive threshold still filters.
- Proved on a real SQLite adapter: a query vector returns both an exact neighbor and an orthogonal one when the threshold is omitted or `0`, and only the exact neighbor at `0.99`.

Provider: xAI
Model: grok-4.6
Client: grok-build

LifeOps Gmail reply send (`POST /api/lifeops/gmail/reply-send` and `GmailDomain.sendGmailReply`, also used by PERSONAL_ASSISTANT resolve-request when `replyToMessageId` is set) defaults the subject when the caller omits `subject`. The default used `/^Re:\\s*/i`. In a regex literal that is one backslash, so it matches `Re:\` plus whitespace, not `Re: `.

On `develop`, `readGmailMessage` already returns subjects such as `Re: Review`. Sending a confirmed reply without `subject` therefore called `sendEmail` with `Re: Re: Review`. First replies (`Quarterly review` → `Re: Quarterly review`) were unaffected because the broken pattern never matched a normal subject.

`plugin-google-workspace` `normalizeReplySubject` cannot correct this: it keeps any subject that already starts with `re:`, including the doubled form.

# Change

- Default LifeOps reply subject uses `/^Re:\s*/i`, the whitespace class the strip-and-prefix intended.
- `GmailDomain.sendGmailReply` without `request.subject` now sends `Re: Review` for an original `Re: Review`, and still sends `Re: Quarterly review` for a first reply.

# Relates to

`XService.fetchConnectorMessages` and `XService.listRecentConnectorTargets` (`plugins/plugin-x/src/services/x.service.ts`) wrapped `listRecentDirectMessages` in `.catch(error => { runtime.reportError(...); return []; })`. Their comment said a DM fetch failure "must surface to the agent rather than reading as an empty inbox". The code returned an empty inbox anyway: an expired token or rate limit read as "no DMs" and "no recent DM partners".

Consumers of that empty result on develop `7d8732d878`:

- **LifeOps X read delegate** (`runtime-service-delegates.ts`, `fetchXDirectMessagesWithRuntimeService`): it already turns a thrown error into `unavailable(...)` (J1). Because nothing was thrown, it returned `{ status: "handled", value: [] }`, so `syncXDms` reported `synced: 0` as success and never took its cache-fallback / delegation-failed path.
- **MESSAGE action** (`plugins/plugin-assistant/.../actions/message.ts`): it already catches and reports a `listRecentTargets` failure, but saw a healthy empty list instead.

The Discord, Slack and Telegram connectors let the same failure propagate.

# Change

Both methods now await `listRecentDirectMessages` directly, so the failure reaches those boundaries. No other behaviour changes.

AI provider/model: anthropic / claude-opus-5-5. Client: Claude Code.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
78:T1120,# Relates to

Every assistant message with a retryable failure (for example `failureKind: "provider_issue"`) shows a Retry button (`MessageContent.tsx`), whatever its position in the transcript. Failure state survives history reloads (#10031), so an old failed reply keeps its button after later successful turns. Clicking it runs `handleChatRetry` (`packages/ui/src/state/useChatSend.ts`), whose retry-in-place path:

1. takes the user message just before the failed reply;
2. drops everything from that message onward locally (`currentMessages.slice(userIdx)`);
3. calls `client.truncateConversationMessages(convId, userMsg.id, { inclusive: true })`, which deletes every memory from that point to the end of the room on the server;
4. resends the earlier question.

So for `[Q1, A1-failed, Q2, A2]`, retrying A1 permanently deletes Q2 and A2 without asking. `ChatOverlay` handles the same case by resending the earlier question as a new turn and deleting nothing.

# Change

When the failed reply has a later user turn and the persisted truncate path would run, `handleChatRetry` resends the earlier question as a new turn at the end (`sendChatText(retryText, { conversationId })`) and removes nothing. When the failed reply is the last turn, the existing in-place retry (truncate, then resend) is unchanged. The local-only fallback for optimistic `temp-` turns already replaces only the selected pair and is also unchanged.

# Testing

`useChatSend.test.tsx` gains a case: seed `[u1, a1 failed, u2, a2]`, retry `a1`, and expect no truncate, no state removal, one resend of `"hello"`, and `u1, a1, u2, a2` still present.

- On develop it fails (`truncateConversationMessages` called once).
- With this change it passes, along with the rest of `useChatSend.test.tsx` (130/130). `bun run test -- src/state src/components/chat` in `packages/ui` passes 97 files and 937 tests.
- `tsc --noEmit` and `biome check` report nothing for the changed files.

79:T11b4,Fixes #33049

# Relates to

Since #32957 (merged 2026-10-01), `createRedactClone` in `packages/core/src/security/log-redaction.ts` gives every plain and Map/Set clone an own non-enumerable `Symbol.toPrimitive`. Node's `console` hides it, but Bun's prints non-enumerable symbol keys, so under Bun (the agent's runtime) every object in a trailing log argument gets an extra line per nesting level:

```ts
logger.info("probe-console-symbol", { owner: { id: "u1" }, tags: new Map([["a", 1]]) });
```

develop `27dec7b0c3`, Bun 1.4.2 terminal:

```
 Info       probe-console-symbol {
  [Symbol(Symbol.toPrimitive)]: [Function: value],
  owner: {
    [Symbol(Symbol.toPrimitive)]: [Function: value],
    id: "u1",
  },
  tags: {
    [Symbol(Symbol.toPrimitive)]: [Function: value],
    type: "Map",
  ...
```

this branch:

```
 Info       probe-console-symbol {
  owner: {
    id: "u1",
  },
  tags: {
    type: "Map",
    entries: [
      [ "a", 1 ]
    ],
  },
}
```

# Change

The hook exists so a clone that owns a non-callable `toString` copied from source data (`{ toString: "source data" }`) still coerces instead of throwing in sinks such as React DevTools' patched console. Without such a key, the inherited `Object.prototype.toString` already answers, and an own `valueOf` alone is harmless because ToPrimitive falls back to `toString`. So:

- plain clones get the hook only when they own `toString` (checked after their properties are copied);
- Map/Set clones never do (their keys are fixed: `type`, `entries`/`values`);
- Error clones keep it unconditionally, as before (Bun prints errors without it).

A shared prototype carrying the hook does not help: Bun prints inherited `Symbol.toPrimitive` too (checked).

# Testing

- [ ] Ran `bun install` and `bun run verify` after syncing; record blockers below.
- [x] Updated relevant documentation (code comments only; no external docs cover this path).

- `bun x vitest run src/security/log-redaction.test.ts` (packages/core): new test asserts plain, nested, array-element, Map, and nested Set clones own no symbol keys and still `String()` to `[object Object]`; a `valueOf`-only clone has none and coerces; a `toString`-shadowing clone keeps the hook. It fails on develop's file (1 failed, 7 passed) and passes here (8/8).
- Mutant "never add the hook": #32957's `preserves non-callable coercion fields…` test and the new test fail, so the crash protection is still pinned.
- `bun x vitest run src/security src/logger.native-esm.test.ts`: 8 files, 46 tests passed. `bun run --cwd packages/core typecheck` clean; Biome clean.

Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com)7c:Ta46,## Summary
- Log pages now break same-timestamp ties by UUID, descending, on both the SQL and SQLite adapters.
- An offset export can no longer repeat or skip a log that shares its neighbor's timestamp.

## Test plan
- [x] `cd plugins/plugin-sqlite && bunx vitest run --config vitest.config.ts adapter.test.ts -t "pages same-millisecond logs"` — 1 passed
- [ ] `bun run verify` — not run; llama.cpp postinstall fails because `xcrun` cannot find `metal`

# Relates to

Log export walks `getLogs` with limit and offset. Ordering only by `createdAt` leaves same-millisecond rows unordered, so a later page can repeat or skip one.

# Change

SQL `getLogs` orders by `createdAt DESC, id DESC`. SQLite applies the same total order with `compareMemoryIds`, which matches PostgreSQL's case-insensitive UUID order. A time-only sort previously kept SQLite's ascending storage-id order.

# Testing
